{"parentUuid":"1b3ab164-1793-4097-86aa-3c79d12a1953","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"f0d327cc-f112-4314-af2e-21469ef6cc93","agentId":"a64c9d9c032371edf","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_015LdkFpfJdySkeCKGS8uCPV","type":"tool_result","content":"=== validateAuth callers ===\nsrc/middleware/auth.ts:96:export async function validateAuth(input: AuthInput, forceRefresh = false): Promise<AuthResult> {\nsrc/routes/post-process.ts:109:  const authResult = await validateAuth({\nsrc/routes/assistant.ts:302:  const authResult = await validateAuth({ licenseKey: licenseKey || undefined });\nsrc/routes/transcribe.ts:396:  const authResult = await validateAuth({\nsrc/routes/ws-streaming-deepgram.ts:154:  const authResult = await validateAuth({ licenseKey });\n=== usage.test.ts ===\nimport { afterEach, describe, expect, mock, test } from 'bun:test';\nimport { Hono } from 'hono';\n\nconst originalFetch = globalThis.fetch;\n\nconst cacheWrites: Array<{ licenseKey: string; license: { isValid: boolean; credits: number; cachedAt: string } }> = [];\n\nmock.module('../lib/redis', () => ({\n  getCachedLicense: async () => ({ isValid: true, credits: 12, cachedAt: 'cached' }),\n  cacheLicense: async (licenseKey: string, license: { isValid: boolean; credits: number; cachedAt: string }) => {\n    cacheWrites.push({ licenseKey, license });\n  },\n  isIPBlocked: async () => false,\n}));\n\nconst { readFiniteCredits, usageRoute } = await import('./usage');\n\nafterEach(() => {\n  cacheWrites.length = 0;\n  globalThis.fetch = originalFetch;\n});\n\ndescribe('readFiniteCredits', () => {\n  test('accepts only finite numeric credits', () => {\n    expect(readFiniteCredits({ credits: 12.5 })).toBe(12.5);\n    expect(readFiniteCredits({ credits: null })).toBeNull();\n    expect(readFiniteCredits({ credits: '12.5' })).toBeNull();\n    expect(readFiniteCredits({ credits: Number.NaN })).toBeNull();\n    expect(readFiniteCredits({ credits: Number.POSITIVE_INFINITY })).toBeNull();\n    expect(readFiniteCredits({})).toBeNull();\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe('usageRoute force refresh', () => {\n  test('does not cache malformed credits balance responses and falls back to validation', async () => {\n    globalThis.fetch = mock(async (input: RequestInfo | URL) => {\n      const url = String(input);\n\n      if (url.includes('/api/license/credits?')) {\n        return Response.json({ credits: null });\n      }\n\n      if (url.includes('/api/license/validate')) {\n        return Response.json({ valid: true, credits: 42 });\n      }\n\n      throw new Error(`Unexpected fetch: ${url}`);\n    }) as unknown as typeof fetch;\n\n    const app = new Hono();\n    app.get('/usage', usageRoute);\n\n    const response = await app.request('/usage?license_key=test-license&force_refresh=true');\n    const body = await response.json() as { credits_remaining: number; is_licensed: boolean };\n\n    expect(response.status).toBe(200);\n    expect(body.is_licensed).toBe(true);\n    expect(body.credits_remaining).toBe(42);\n    expect(cacheWrites).toHaveLength(1);\n    expect(cacheWrites[0]?.license).toMatchObject({ isValid: true, credits: 42 });","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"07ad36fa-7f5c-4dc3-bf9d-e94c83875f8b","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:36:56.615Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"=== validateAuth callers ===\nsrc/middleware/auth.ts:96:export async function validateAuth(input: AuthInput, forceRefresh = false): Promise<AuthResult> {\nsrc/routes/post-process.ts:109:  const authResult = await validateAuth({\nsrc/routes/assistant.ts:302:  const authResult = await validateAuth({ licenseKey: licenseKey || undefined });\nsrc/routes/transcribe.ts:396:  const authResult = await validateAuth({\nsrc/routes/ws-streaming-deepgram.ts:154:  const authResult = await validateAuth({ licenseKey });\n=== usage.test.ts ===\nimport { afterEach, describe, expect, mock, test } from 'bun:test';\nimport { Hono } from 'hono';\n\nconst originalFetch = globalThis.fetch;\n\nconst cacheWrites: Array<{ licenseKey: string; license: { isValid: boolean; credits: number; cachedAt: string } }> = [];\n\nmock.module('../lib/redis', () => ({\n  getCachedLicense: async () => ({ isValid: true, credits: 12, cachedAt: 'cached' }),\n  cacheLicense: async (licenseKey: string, license: { isValid: boolean; credits: number; cachedAt: string }) => {\n    cacheWrites.push({ licenseKey, license });\n  },\n  isIPBlocked: async () => false,\n}));\n\nconst { readFiniteCredits, usageRoute } = await import('./usage');\n\nafterEach(() => {\n  cacheWrites.length = 0;\n  globalThis.fetch = originalFetch;\n});\n\ndescribe('readFiniteCredits', () => {\n  test('accepts only finite numeric credits', () => {\n    expect(readFiniteCredits({ credits: 12.5 })).toBe(12.5);\n    expect(readFiniteCredits({ credits: null })).toBeNull();\n    expect(readFiniteCredits({ credits: '12.5' })).toBeNull();\n    expect(readFiniteCredits({ credits: Number.NaN })).toBeNull();\n    expect(readFiniteCredits({ credits: Number.POSITIVE_INFINITY })).toBeNull();\n    expect(readFiniteCredits({})).toBeNull();\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe('usageRoute force refresh', () => {\n  test('does not cache malformed credits balance responses and falls back to validation', async () => {\n    globalThis.fetch = mock(async (input: RequestInfo | URL) => {\n      const url = String(input);\n\n      if (url.includes('/api/license/credits?')) {\n        return Response.json({ credits: null });\n      }\n\n      if (url.includes('/api/license/validate')) {\n        return Response.json({ valid: true, credits: 42 });\n      }\n\n      throw new Error(`Unexpected fetch: ${url}`);\n    }) as unknown as typeof fetch;\n\n    const app = new Hono();\n    app.get('/usage', usageRoute);\n\n    const response = await app.request('/usage?license_key=test-license&force_refresh=true');\n    const body = await response.json() as { credits_remaining: number; {"parentUuid":"fa9c979c-2ccc-4d0c-96e2-19ae1e6edf8f","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a64c9d9c032371edf","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdWHG4o2MzP5TRMEArLA2","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Investigation complete. Both oracle signals resolved.\n\n---\n\n**CANDIDATE finding**\n\nfile: `/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/hyperwhisper-cloud/src/routes/usage.ts`\nline: 48\nsummary: `/usage`'s hand-rolled licence validator writes `isValid:false` into the shared `license:<key>` Redis cache on *any* non-200 response including 5xx, the exact transient-failure case `validateAuth` deliberately refuses to cache, so one upstream blip locks a paying user out of every other route for a full hour.\nfailure_scenario: The licensing API returns 503 (Fly/Vercel cold start, deploy, upstream timeout) while a client polls `GET /usage?license_key=K`. `validateLicenseAndGetCredits` never inspects `response.status` — `const data = await response.json().catch(() => ({}))` (line 44) turns the 503 body into `{}`, `data.valid === true` is false (line 45), and line 48 unconditionally does `await cacheLicense(licenseKey, { isValid: false, credits: 0, ... })` with `ex: LICENSE_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS` = **3600s** (`src/lib/constants.ts:7`, `// 1 hour for valid and invalid keys`). For the next hour every `/transcribe`, `/post-process`, `/assistant` and `/ws/streaming-deepgram` request from that paying customer hits `validateAuth` → `getCachedLicense` → `if (!cached.isValid) return { ok: false, response: invalidLicenseResponse() }` (`src/middleware/auth.ts:113-115`) and 401s, *even after the licensing API recovers seconds later*. No route can escape it: every one of the four call sites invokes `validateAuth({ licenseKey })` with the default `forceRefresh = false`, so nothing bypasses the poisoned entry. This is verbatim the harm `auth.ts:63-73` documents and guards against — *\"A 5xx … is a transient failure, not proof the license is invalid — caching it would lock a paying user out for the full LICENSE_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS … Fail this request closed but leave the cache untouched\"* — enforced there by `if (response.status >= 500) { … return { isValid: false, credits: 0 }; }` (auth.ts:70-73) and **absent entirely** from the `usage.ts` copy. (`usage.ts`'s `catch` block at 55-57 *does* correctly skip caching on network/timeout errors, which shows the omission is specific to the HTTP-status path, not a blanket oversight.)\ntrigger_path: `GET /usage?license_key=K` → `src/index.ts:54` `app.get('/usage', usageRoute)` → `usageRoute` (usage.ts:93) → cache miss → `validateLicenseAndGetCredits(K, false)` (usage.ts:128) → `fetch(.../api/license/validate)` returns 5xx → **usage.ts:48** `cacheLicense(K, {isValid:false})` → *(next hour, any route)* → `validateAuth` (auth.ts:110) → `getCachedLicense` → auth.ts:113 → 401.\noracle: [D2]\n\n---\n\n**CANDIDATE finding**\n\nfile: `/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/hyperwhisper-cloud/src/lib/cost-calculator.ts`\nline: 499\nsummary: The live money conversion `creditsForCost` returns a `0.1` credit floor for `costUsd <= 0` while its dead twin `estimateCreditsForCost` returns `0` for the same input; two downstream guards were written against the dead function's contract, so a zero-cost streaming session still bills the user 0.1 credits.\nfailure_scenario: A client opens `wss://…/ws/streaming-deepgram?license_key=K`, clears preflight auth+credits, then closes before sending any PCM (user taps the hotkey twice, or a mobile/wifi drop kills the socket mid-handshake). `endSession` runs with `totalDurationSeconds = 0` → `computeDeepgramTranscriptionCost(0)` = `0` → `creditsForCost(0)` takes the `costUsd <= 0` branch and **returns `0.1`** (cost-calculator.ts:498-499). The intended stop, `if (creditsUsed > 0)` at `ws-streaming-deepgram.ts:208`, is therefore *dead* — `creditsForCost` can never return ≤ 0 — so `deductCredits(auth, 0, { audio_duration_seconds: 0, transcription_cost_usd: 0 }, …)` fires; inside, `performDeduction` recomputes `creditsForCost(0)` = `0.1` and the second dead guard `if (creditsUsed <= 0) return 0;` (`middleware/credits.ts:140`) also fails to stop it, so `recordLicenseUsage(K, 0.1, …)` POSTs a real 0.1-credit charge for zero seconds of audio. The client is simultaneously told `{ type: 'session_complete', duration_seconds: 0, credits_used: 0.1 }` (ws:203-205). A flaky-network client reconnecting 100 times burns 10 credits (~6 minutes of paid transcription) having transcribed nothing. `estimateCreditsForCost` — the *dead* function, zero importers — implements the contract both guards assume (`if (!Number.isFinite(costUsd) || costUsd <= 0) return 0;`, line 506-508); it and `creditsForCost` are byte-identical apart from that return value. The three live call sites hold three different beliefs about the same function: `assistant.ts:351` guards correctly on `if (costUsd > 0)`; `transcribe.ts:676` needed a bespoke `noSpeech ? 0 : creditsForCost(...)` workaround plus an `if (!noSpeech)` wrapper to dodge the floor; `ws-streaming-deepgram.ts:208` and `credits.ts:140` guard on the credits value and get it wrong. Compounding it: `src/lib/cost-calculator.test.ts` imports only the eight `compute*` / `estimatePromptInputReservationUsd` functions (test file lines 2-11) — `creditsForCost`, `usdToCredits`, `estimateCreditsForCost` and `roundUsd` have **no assertions anywhere**; `transcribe.test.ts` calls `creditsForCost` only to build expected values on both sides of an `expect`, never to pin its behaviour, and never near the zero boundary. The money conversion that decides what every user is charged is the one function the money-conversion test file does not test.\ntrigger_path: `GET /ws/streaming-deepgram?license_key=K` (Upgrade) → `src/index.ts:57` → `wsStreamingPreflight` (passes) → `wsStreamingRoute` → socket closes with 0 bytes sent → `endSession` (ws-streaming-deepgram.ts:186) → `creditsForCost(0)` → **cost-calculator.ts:499** returns `0.1` → dead guard ws:208 passes → `deductCredits` → `performDeduction` (credits.ts:138) → dead guard credits.ts:140 passes → `recordLicenseUsage(K, 0.1)`.\noracle: [D2]\n\n---\n\n**BENIGN**\n\n- **`usage.ts` skipping `validateAuth`/`validateCredits` is not an auth hole.** It rolls its own check but still fails closed on both ends: unknown/absent key → `return errorResponse(401, 'License required', …)` (usage.ts:152), and invalid key → `if (!isValid) return errorResponse(401, 'Invalid license key', …)` (usage.ts:133-135). It also applies the *same* IP guard as all four siblings — `if (await isIPBlocked(clientIP)) return errorResponse(403, …)` (usage.ts:96) matches transcribe.ts:276 / post-process.ts:64 / assistant.ts:247 / ws:137. Skipping `validateCredits` is correct: `/usage` consumes nothing and calls no provider.\n- **The oracle's implied \"usage.ts skips rate limiting / IP checks / key-format checks the siblings apply\" is false.** `validateAuth` applies none of those either — it is purely `cache → fetch → verdict`. `grep -rn \"rateLimit|RATE_LIMIT\"` over `src/` returns **zero hits**: there is no rate limiting anywhere in the service, and `src/index.ts` wires no global middleware except `cors`. So `/usage` is not *asymmetrically* under-protected; a licence-key enumeration oracle (401 vs 200-with-balance) exists identically on all five routes, `/usage` merely being the cheapest to drive (bodiless GET). That is a codebase-wide absence, not the [D2] guard gap, and no line of code claims otherwise.\n- **`/usage` returns only the caller's own balance for a key the caller already supplied** (`credits_remaining`, `minutes_remaining`, `credits_per_minute`, `is_licensed/is_trial/is_anonymous`, usage.ts:140-147) — no account identity, email, or cross-tenant data. No disclosure beyond \"this key is valid and has N credits\", which is the same fact `/transcribe` leaks via its 401-vs-200.\n- **`getCreditsBalance` caching `isValid: true` (usage.ts:81-85) is not a revocation bypass introduced here.** It is gated behind an already-valid cache entry (`if (cached?.isValid)`, usage.ts:112) and only writes on `response.ok`, so a revoked key is rejected upstream. `credits.ts:86-91` does the identical thing on every successful deduction, so validity-renewal-from-the-credits-endpoint is the established codebase-wide contract, not a `usage.ts` divergence.\n- **`roundUsd`** — false positive: 20+ call sites *inside* `cost-calculator.ts` (every `compute*` return). Over-exported, not dead.\n- **`usdToCredits`** — used within its own file only, by `creditsForCost:501` and `estimateCreditsForCost:510`. Not dead; its own `usd <= 0 → 0.1` branch (line 485-487) is the root of Candidate 2.\n- **`estimateCreditsForCost`** — genuinely dead. Zero callers in `src/`, zero test coverage. Two CANDIDATEs, ten BENIGN. Verbatim blocks below.\n\nCANDIDATE finding\nfile: /Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/hyperwhisper-cloud/src/routes/usage.ts\nline: 48\nsummary: `/usage`'s hand-rolled licence validator writes `isValid:false` into the shared `license:<key>` Redis cache on *any* non-200 response including 5xx, the exact transient-failure case `validateAuth` deliberately refuses to cache, so one upstream blip locks a paying user out of every other route for a full hour.\nfailure_scenario: The licensing API returns 503 (Fly/Vercel cold start, deploy, upstream timeout) while a client polls `GET /usage?license_key=K`. `validateLicenseAndGetCredits` never inspects `response.status` — `const data = await response.json().catch(() => ({}))` (line 44) turns the 503 body into `{}`, `data.valid === true` is false (line 45), and line 48 unconditionally does `await cacheLicense(licenseKey, { isValid: false, credits: 0, ... })` with `ex: LICENSE_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS` = 3600s (src/lib/constants.ts:7, `// 1 hour for valid and invalid keys`). For the next hour every /transcribe, /post-process, /assistant and /ws/streaming-deepgram request from that paying customer hits `validateAuth` → `getCachedLicense` → `if (!cached.isValid) return { ok: false, response: invalidLicenseResponse() }` (src/middleware/auth.ts:113-115) and 401s, even after the licensing API recovers seconds later. No route can escape it: all four call sites invoke `validateAuth({ licenseKey })` with the default `forceRefresh = false`, so nothing bypasses the poisoned entry — the only force-refresh path in the service is `/usage?force_refresh=true` itself. This is verbatim the harm auth.ts:63-73 documents and guards against — \"A 5xx … is a transient failure, not proof the license is invalid — caching it would lock a paying user out for the full LICENSE_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS … Fail this request closed but leave the cache untouched\" — enforced there by `if (response.status >= 500) { … return { isValid: false, credits: 0 }; }` (auth.ts:70-73) and absent entirely from the usage.ts copy. (usage.ts's `catch` block at 55-57 does correctly skip caching on network/timeout errors, showing the omission is specific to the HTTP-status path, not a blanket oversight.)\ntrigger_path: `GET /usage?license_key=K` → src/index.ts:54 `app.get('/usage', usageRoute)` → `usageRoute` (usage.ts:93) → cache miss → `validateLicenseAndGetCredits(K, false)` (usage.ts:128) → `fetch(.../api/license/validate)` returns 5xx → usage.ts:48 `cacheLicense(K, {isValid:false})` → (next hour, any route) → `validateAuth` (auth.ts:110) → `getCachedLicense` → auth.ts:113 → 401.\noracle: [D2]\n\nCANDIDATE finding\nfile: /Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/hyperwhisper-cloud/src/lib/cost-calculator.ts\nline: 499\nsummary: The live money conversion `creditsForCost` returns a `0.1` credit floor for `costUsd <= 0` while its dead twin `estimateCreditsForCost` returns `0` for the same input; two downstream guards were written against the dead function's contract, so a zero-cost streaming session still bills the user 0.1 credits.\nfailure_scenario: A client opens `wss://…/ws/streaming-deepgram?license_key=K`, clears preflight auth+credits, then closes before sending any PCM (user taps the hotkey twice, or a mobile/wifi drop kills the socket mid-handshake). `endSession` runs with `totalDurationSeconds = 0` → `computeDeepgramTranscriptionCost(0)` = 0 → `creditsForCost(0)` takes the `costUsd <= 0` branch and returns `0.1` (cost-calculator.ts:498-499). The intended stop, `if (creditsUsed > 0)` at ws-streaming-deepgram.ts:208, is therefore dead — `creditsForCost` can never return ≤ 0 — so `deductCredits(auth, 0, { audio_duration_seconds: 0, transcription_cost_usd: 0 }, …)` fires; inside, `performDeduction` recomputes `creditsForCost(0)` = 0.1 and the second dead guard `if (creditsUsed <= 0) return 0;` (middleware/credits.ts:140) also fails to stop it, so `recordLicenseUsage(K, 0.1, …)` POSTs a real 0.1-credit charge for zero seconds of audio. The client is simultaneously told `{ type: 'session_complete', duration_seconds: 0, credits_used: 0.1 }` (ws:203-205). A flaky-network client reconnecting 100 times burns 10 credits (~6 minutes of paid transcription) having transcribed nothing. `estimateCreditsForCost` — the dead function, zero importers — implements the contract both guards assume (`if (!Number.isFinite(costUsd) || costUsd <= 0) return 0;`, lines 506-508); it and `creditsForCost` are byte-identical apart from that return value. The three live call sites hold three different beliefs about the same function: assistant.ts:351 guards correctly on `if (costUsd > 0)`; transcribe.ts:676 needed a bespoke `noSpeech ? 0 : creditsForCost(...)` workaround plus an `if (!noSpeech)` wrapper to dodge the floor; ws-streaming-deepgram.ts:208 and credits.ts:140 guard on the credits value and get it wrong. Compounding it: src/lib/cost-calculator.test.ts imports only the eight `compute*` / `estimatePromptInputReservationUsd` functions (test file lines 2-11) — `creditsForCost`, `usdToCredits`, `estimateCreditsForCost` and `roundUsd` have no assertions anywhere; transcribe.test.ts calls `creditsForCost` only to build expected values on both sides of an `expect`, never to pin its behaviour, and never near the zero boundary. The money conversion that decides what every user is charged is the one function the money-conversion test file does not test.\ntrigger_path: `GET /ws/streaming-deepgram?license_key=K` (Upgrade) → src/index.ts:57 → `wsStreamingPreflight` (passes) → `wsStreamingRoute` → socket closes with 0 bytes sent → `endSession` (ws-streaming-deepgram.ts:186) → `creditsForCost(0)` → cost-calculator.ts:499 returns 0.1 → dead guard ws:208 passes → `deductCredits` → `performDeduction` (credits.ts:138) → dead guard credits.ts:140 passes → `recordLicenseUsage(K, 0.1)`.\noracle: [D2]\n\nBENIGN:\n- usage.ts skipping validateAuth/validateCredits is not an auth hole: it fails closed on both ends — `return errorResponse(401, 'License required', …)` (usage.ts:152) and `if (!isValid) return errorResponse(401, 'Invalid license key', …)` (usage.ts:133-135) — and applies the same IP guard as all four siblings, `if (await isIPBlocked(clientIP)) return errorResponse(403, …)` (usage.ts:96) matching transcribe.ts:276 / post-process.ts:64 / assistant.ts:247 / ws:137. Skipping validateCredits is correct: /usage consumes nothing and calls no provider.\n- The implied \"usage.ts skips rate limiting / IP checks / key-format checks the siblings apply\" is false: validateAuth applies none of those either — it is purely cache → fetch → verdict. `grep -rn \"rateLimit|RATE_LIMIT\"` over src/ returns zero hits; src/index.ts wires no global middleware except `cors`. A licence-key enumeration oracle (401 vs 200-with-balance) exists identically on all five routes, /usage merely being the cheapest to drive (bodiless GET). That is a codebase-wide absence, not the [D2] guard gap.\n- /usage returns only the caller's own balance for a key the caller already supplied (`credits_remaining`, `minutes_remaining`, `credits_per_minute`, `is_licensed/is_trial/is_anonymous`, usage.ts:140-147) — no account identity, email, or cross-tenant data.\n- `getCreditsBalance` caching `isValid: true` (usage.ts:81-85) is not a revocation bypass introduced here: gated behind an already-valid cache entry (`if (cached?.isValid)`, usage.ts:112) and only written on `response.ok`. credits.ts:86-91 does the identical thing on every successful deduction, so validity-renewal-from-the-credits-endpoint is the established codebase-wide contract, not a usage.ts divergence.\n- Auth cache TTL: `LICENSE_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS = 60 * 60` (src/lib/constants.ts:7), applied at src/lib/redis.ts:72 as `set('license:<key>', …, { ex: LICENSE_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS })`. One hour, and — per the constant's own comment — the same TTL for valid and invalid keys. Negative results ARE handled deliberately in auth.ts (4xx cached on purpose to stop bad keys hammering the licensing API, 5xx/network explicitly not cached), so the cache design itself is sound; only usage.ts's copy breaks it. Consequence of the TTL as designed: a revoked key stays valid for up to 3600s, since no route ever calls validateAuth with forceRefresh=true.\n- `roundUsd` — false positive: 20+ call sites inside cost-calculator.ts (every compute* return). Over-exported, not dead.\n- `usdToCredits` — used within its own file only, by creditsForCost:501 and estimateCreditsForCost:510. Its `usd <= 0 → 0.1` branch (485-487) is the root of Candidate 2.\n- `estimateCreditsForCost` — genuinely dead. Zero callers in src/, zero test coverage. It is the correct implementation stranded next to the buggy live one (Candidate 2).\n- `tryExtractCorrectionText` — false positive: recursive, six self-calls in text-processing.ts (44, 52, 61, 66, 106, 120) plus its entry point at 142.\n- `ALL_STT_PROVIDER_IDS` (stt-models.ts:216) — genuinely dead: sole occurrence in the repo is its own definition. Harmless unused export, no behavioural consequence.\n- `estimatePromptTextTokens` (gemini.ts:155) — false positive: called at gemini.ts:250.\n- `INLINE_AUDIO_MAX_BYTES` (google-chirp.ts:33) — false positive: used at lines 132, 146, 149 in the same file.","type":"message","recipient":"oracle-hunter","content":"Lead [D2] complete. No files were modified (read-only respected).\n\nConclusion for lead [D2] — 2 CANDIDATEs, 12 BENIGN lines:\n\n**CANDIDATE 1 — `src/routes/usage.ts:48`.** `/usage` rolls its own licence validator and caches `isValid:false` on *any* non-200 response, including 5xx — the exact transient case `validateAuth` deliberately refuses to cache (`if (response.status >= 500) { ... return { isValid: false, credits: 0 }; }`, `src/middleware/auth.ts:70-73`, with a comment saying caching it \"would lock a paying user out for the full LICENSE_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS\"). One 503 from the licensing API during a `GET /usage` poll poisons the shared `license:<key>` Redis entry for 3600s, and every sibling route then 401s via `auth.ts:113` even after the API recovers. Nothing can bypass it: all four `validateAuth` call sites use the default `forceRefresh = false`.\n\n**CANDIDATE 2 — `src/lib/cost-calculator.ts:499`.** The live money conversion `creditsForCost` returns a `0.1` floor for `costUsd <= 0`; the dead `estimateCreditsForCost` returns `0` for the same input. Two downstream guards (`ws-streaming-deepgram.ts:208`, `credits.ts:140`) were written against the *dead* function's contract and are therefore unreachable, so a WebSocket session that sends zero audio bills the user 0.1 credits and reports `duration_seconds: 0, credits_used: 0.1`. `transcribe.ts:676` carries a bespoke `noSpeech ? 0 : ...` workaround for the same floor while `assistant.ts:351` guards correctly on `costUsd > 0` — three call sites, three different beliefs. `cost-calculator.test.ts` covers only the eight `compute*` functions; the credit conversion has no assertions anywhere.\n\n**Guard-asymmetry verdict:** the raw signal is misleading. `/usage` fails closed on both paths (401 at lines 133-135 and 152) and applies the same `isIPBlocked` guard as its siblings. There is no rate limiting anywhere in the service (zero `rateLimit`/`RATE_LIMIT` hits; `index.ts` wires only `cors`), so the licence-key enumeration oracle exists identically on all five routes — a codebase-wide absence, not a `usage.ts` hole. The real asymmetry is narrower and worse: the duplicated validator, not the missing middleware.\n\n**Auth cache TTL:** `LICENSE_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS = 60 * 60` (`constants.ts:7`), applied at `redis.ts:72`, same TTL for valid and invalid keys.
